{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is the main component of the cone
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
(see
Definition

7.3.2
). We then define reduced invariants (see Definition
7.4.4
) via an obstruction theory on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
relative to
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}
(see Theorem
7.5.3
).

We also recall maps with fields
[
CL12
]
and then we construct a blow-up of it which makes the resulting stack as simple as possible. The resulting stack gives an alternative definition of reduced invariants, which is not intrinsic; the relation between these two invariants is similar in spirit to a Quantum Lefschetz theorem. The definition we give is more intrinsic, but working with maps with fields instead of maps is more suited to approaching
1.0.2
and
1.0.3
. See
[
CL15
,
LO22
,
LO21
]
for the proof of
1.0.3
in genus one and two.

7.1.
Stable maps as open in an abelian cone

We recall how the moduli space of stable maps to projective space can be seen as an open substack of an abelian cone, following
[
CL12
]
. This observation motivates our study of components of cones in
Section

6
, as components of the ambient abelian cone are related to components of stable maps.

Let
\mathfrak{M}_{g,n}
denote the stack of genus
g
pre-stable curves with
n
marked points, that is,
\mathfrak{M}_{g,n}
parametrizes connected projective at-worst-nodal curves of arithmetic genus
g
with
n
distinct smooth marked points. Let
\underline{\mathfrak{C}}_{g,n}
denote universal curve over
\mathfrak{M}_{g,n}
.
Let
\mathfrak{Pic}_{g,n,d}
denote the Artin stack which parameterises genus
g
pre-stable curves, with
n
marked points, together with a line bundle of degree
d
. Let
\mathfrak{Pic}_{g,n,d}^{\rm st}
denote the open subset of
\mathfrak{Pic}_{g,n,d}
consisting of
(C,p_{1}\ldots p_{n},L)
which satisfy the stability condition

(23)

L^{\otimes 3}\otimes\omega_{C}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}\right)\mbox{ is ample.}

Notice that
\mathfrak{M}_{g,n}
and
\mathfrak{Pic}_{g,n,d}
are not separated, but they are smooth (see
[
Sta22
, Lemma 0E6W]
and
[
CFKM14
, Proposition 2.11]
) and irreducible. The stack
\mathfrak{Pic}_{g,n,d}
is locally Noetherian and the stack
\mathfrak{Pic}_{g,n,d}^{\rm st}
is Noetherian.

Notation 7.1.1
.

From now on, we fix
g,n,d
and the stability condition and we drop all the indices.

We define
\mathfrak{C}
the universal curve over
\mathfrak{Pic}
by the Cartesian diagram (
24
). Notice that we also have a universal line bundle
\mathfrak{L}
over
\mathfrak{C}
.

(24)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathfrak{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}\text{.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

We form the cone of sections of
\mathfrak{L}
as in Chang-Li (
[
CL15
]
,
Section 2)

(25)

S(\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}):=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,(R^{1}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}))\to\mathfrak{Pic}.

In the following we collect a list of remarks on the cone of sections defined above.

(1)

In
[
CL12
, Proposition 2.2]
, the authors show that
S(\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})
is the moduli stack parameterizing
(C,L,s)
with
(C,L)\in\mathfrak{Pic}
and
{s}\in H^{0}(C,L)
. Be aware that our
S(\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})
is denoted by
C(\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})
in
[
CL12
]
.

(2)

This situation is similar to the discussion in
Section

2.3
about the total space of a locally free sheaf. If
\mathfrak{E}
is a locally free sheaf over
\mathfrak{Pic}
, then sections of
\mathfrak{E}
correspond to sections of the vector bundle
\mathrm{Tot}(\mathfrak{E})=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,(\mathfrak{E}^{\vee})
over
\mathfrak{Pic}
, but the same is not true if
\mathfrak{E}
is not locally free.

(3)

In our set-up, the sheaf
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}
is not locally free. However, since we work with the universal family of curves
\mathfrak{C}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
, sections of the sheaf
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}
correspond to sections of the abelian cone of its
Serre dual

R^{1}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}})
.
This is proven in
[
CL12
, Proposition 2.2]
.

(4)

Note that
R^{0}\pi_{\ast}\mathfrak{L}
does not commute with base change but
R^{1}\pi_{\ast}\mathfrak{L}
does by cohomology and base change.

For the rest of the section, let

(26)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}:=R^{1}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}).

Note that since
\pi
is proper, we have that
\mathfrak{F}
is a coherent sheaf on
\mathfrak{Pic}
. As defined in
Section

2.3
, we consider the stack
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,\mathfrak{F}
, which is an abelian cone stack over
\mathfrak{Pic}
.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
be the moduli space of genus
g
, degree
d
stable maps, with
n
marked points.

Proposition 7.1.2
.

(
[
CL12
, Proposition 2.7]
,
[
CFK10
, Theorem 3.2.1]
)
The moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is an open substack, cut out by the basepoint-free condition, of the stack

(27)

\displaystyle S(\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\oplus r+1})=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathfrak{F})\to\mathfrak{Pic}.

As before, a point of this cone over
(C,L)\in\mathfrak{Pic}
is
(C,L,\underline{s})
with
\underline{s}\in H^{0}(C,L)^{\oplus r+1}
. Note that

S(\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\oplus r+1})=\overbrace{S(\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})\times_{\mathfrak{Pic}}\cdots\times_{\mathfrak{Pic}}S(\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})}^{r+1\rm\ times}\to\mathfrak{Pic}.

We define
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{C}
by the following Cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S(\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\oplus r+1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}\text{.}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\pi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

By
[
CL12
]
, the complex

\oplus_{i=0}^{r}R^{\bullet}\overline{\pi}_{*}\mathcal{L}

is a dual obstruction theory for the natural projection

\mu:S(\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\oplus r+1})=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathfrak{F})\to\mathfrak{Pic}.

This perfect obstruction theory induces a virtual class

[\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}:=\mu^{!}[\mathfrak{Pic}]\in A_{*}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)).

Proposition 7.1.3
.

We use
7.1.1
. For
\mathfrak{F}
defined in
eq.

26
, we have an isomorphism of sheaves

\mathfrak{F}^{\vee}:=\left(R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\right)^{\vee}\simeq\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}

over
\mathfrak{Pic}
.

Proof.

Using Grothendieck duality we have

\displaystyle\left(R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\right)^{\vee}

\displaystyle=R\mathcal{H}om_{\mathfrak{Pic}}(R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Pic}})

\displaystyle=R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}R\mathcal{H}om_{\mathfrak{Pic}}(\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}},\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}[1])

\displaystyle=R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}R\mathcal{H}om_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{C}},\mathfrak{L}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}[1])

\displaystyle=R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}R\mathcal{H}om_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{C}},\mathfrak{L}[1])

(28)

\displaystyle=R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}[1].

On the one hand, we have that

(29)

h^{-1}(R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}[1])=\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}.

In the following we look at an explicit resolution of
\left(R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\right)^{\vee}
and compute its
h^{-1}
. This is similar to the discussion in
[
CFK20
]
, Section 3.2. By the stability condition on
\mathfrak{Pic}
(see
Equation

23
), the universal curve over
\mathfrak{Pic}
is projective. This ensures that we have an ample section on
\mathfrak{C}
and we take
A
sufficiently large so that
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}(-A)\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}=0
.

We now consider the exact sequence of sheaves on the universal curve over
\mathfrak{Pic}

0\to\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}(-A)\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\to\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\to\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}|_{A}\to 0.

Pushing forward the above to
\mathfrak{Pic}
, we get a long exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\to

\displaystyle R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}|_{A}\to

(30)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}(-A)\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\to 0.

This gives

(31)

R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\simeq[R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}|_{A}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}(-A)\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}],

with the complex on the right, being a complex of vector bundles supported in
[0,1]
. This gives

(R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}})^{\vee}\simeq[(R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}(-A)\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}})^{\vee}\to(R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}|_{A})^{\vee}],

with the complex on the right being supported in
[-1,0]
.

Applying the functor
\mathcal{H}om(-,\mathcal{O})
to (
7.1
) and using that it is left-exact, we get

(32)

0\to\left(R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\right)^{\vee}\to(R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}(-A)\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}})^{\vee}\to(R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}|_{A})^{\vee}

This together with
eq.

31
shows that

(33)

h^{-1}\left(\left(R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\right)^{\vee}\right)=\left(R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\right)^{\vee}.

Sections

7.1
,
29
and
33
imply that

\left(R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\right)^{\vee}=R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}.\qed

Remark 7.1.4
.

As in the proof of
Proposition

7.1.3
, we have an explicit resolution of
\mathfrak{F}
(see
[
CFK20
]
, Section 3.2). Let
A
be a sufficiently high power of a very ample section of the morphism
\mathfrak{C}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
such that
R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)=0
. The short exact sequence

(34)

0\to\mathfrak{L}\to\mathfrak{L}(A)\to\mathfrak{L}(A)|_{A}\to 0.

induces a long exact sequence

(35)

0\to R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}\to R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)\to R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)|_{A}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}\to 0,

which shows that
[R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)\to R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)|_{A}]
is quasi-isomorphic to
R^{\bullet}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}
.

With our choice of
A
we have that
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)
and
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)|_{A}
are locally free sheaves over
\mathfrak{Pic}
.
Sequence (
35
) together with the fact that
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)
is a locally free sheaf over
\mathfrak{Pic}
implies that
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}
is a torsion-free sheaf on
\mathfrak{Pic}
.

Remark 7.1.5
.

The proof of
Proposition

7.1.3
shows that we have a resolution of
\mathfrak{F}:=R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}
to the left given by (
7.1
). The isomorphism in
Section

7.1
shows that the complex

[R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}|_{A}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}(-A)\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}]

is dual to

[R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)\to R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)|_{A}].

We will use this duality in
Section

9
. Hu and Li work with the resolution to the right we have in (
35
). In the previous sections we used the resolution to the left
Equation

35
. Since dual morphisms have the same Fitting ideals, both morphisms give the same Hu–Li blow-up.

7.2.
The main component of stable maps to
\mathbb{P}^{r}

In the following we look at stable maps with a lower bound on the degree (see Assumption
7.2.1
). In this situation the moduli space of stable maps has a
main (irreducible) component
. We discuss this main component and its relation to the main component of abelian cones.

We fix the following assumption from now on.

Assumption 7.2.1
.

In the following we fix
d>2g-2
. For
C
a smooth genus
g
curve,
L
a line bundle of degree
d
, and
d>2g-2
, we have that
H^{1}(C,L)=0
. This shows that for
d>2g-2
, the locus of stable maps with smooth domain is smooth and irreducible, so its closure is an irreducible component of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
.

Definition 7.2.2

(Main Component)
.

Consider the Zariski closure in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
of the locus where the curve is smooth of genus
g
. We call this component the
main component
and we denote it by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
.

We introduced the main component of an abelian cone in
Definition

6.1.5
. In our next result,
Proposition

7.2.3
, we show that the main component of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is contained in the main component of
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathfrak{F})
. By the proof of
proposition

7.2.3
, on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
the universal curve is generically smooth and
\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}
is generically a vector bundle.

Proposition 7.2.3
.

We have that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is an open substack of

\mathrm{Spec}\,(\mathrm{Sym}\,\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathfrak{F})^{\mathrm{tf}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
and
\mathfrak{Pic}^{\mathrm{sm}}
denote the open substacks of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
and
\mathfrak{Pic}
where the curve is smooth. The first step is to show that the sheaf
\mathfrak{F}=R^{1}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}})
is locally free over
\mathfrak{Pic}^{\mathrm{sm}}
.

Let
\pi^{\mathrm{sm}}\colon\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}\to\mathfrak{Pic}^{\mathrm{sm}}
denote the universal curve and
\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{sm}}
the universal line bundle on
\mathfrak{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}
. Assumption
7.2.1
and cohomology and base change ensure that
R^{1}\pi^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\ast}\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{sm}}=0
.
Therefore,
R^{0}\pi^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\ast}\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{sm}}
has constant rank, so it locally free. Using Serre Duality and local freeness of
\omega_{\pi^{\mathrm{sm}}}
, we see that

R^{1}\pi^{\mathrm{sm}}_{*}(\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{sm}\vee}\otimes\omega_{\pi^{\mathrm{sm}}})\simeq(R^{0}\pi^{\mathrm{sm}}_{*}((\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{sm}\vee}\otimes\omega_{\pi^{\mathrm{sm}}})^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\pi^{\mathrm{sm}}}))^{\vee}\simeq(R^{0}\pi^{\mathrm{sm}}_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\vee}

is locally free.

In particular, the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Pic}}
-algebra
(\mathrm{Sym}\,\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathfrak{F})^{\mathrm{tf}}
is locally free over
\mathfrak{Pic}^{\mathrm{sm}}
.

To simplify the notation, let
C=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathfrak{F}
and
C^{\mathrm{tf}}=\mathrm{Spec}\,(\mathrm{Sym}\,\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathfrak{F})^{\mathrm{tf}}
. By
Proposition

6.1.2
and using that
\mathfrak{Pic}^{\mathrm{sm}}
is generically reduced, we have that

(36)

\mathrm{cl}^{\mathrm{sch}}_{C}(\pi^{-1}(\mathfrak{Pic}^{\mathrm{sm}}))=C^{\mathrm{tf}},

where
\pi^{-1}(\mathfrak{Pic}^{\mathrm{sm}})
denotes the fibre product
\mathfrak{Pic}^{\mathrm{sm}}\times_{\mathfrak{Pic}}C
, which is open in
C
.

We conclude by the following chain of equalities

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{cl}^{\mathrm{sch}}_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}(\pi^{-1}(\mathfrak{Pic}^{\mathrm{sm}})\times_{C}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d))=

\displaystyle=\mathrm{cl}^{\mathrm{sch}}_{C}(\pi^{-1}(\mathfrak{Pic}^{\mathrm{sm}}))\times_{C}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)=

\displaystyle=C^{\mathrm{tf}}\times_{C}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d).

The first equality is the definition of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
, the second one is
Lemma

7.2.4
(applied to
Y=C
and
W,V
reduced open subschemes of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
and
\pi^{-1}(\mathfrak{Pic}^{\mathrm{sm}})
respectively), and the last one is
Equation

36
.
∎

Lemma 7.2.4
.

Let
Y
be a scheme, let
V,W
be reduced open subschemes of
Y
. Then

\mathrm{cl}^{\mathrm{sch}}_{W}(V\times_{Y}W)=\mathrm{cl}^{\mathrm{sch}}_{Y}(V)\times_{Y}W

Proof.

Since
V
and
W
are reduced, so is
V\times_{Y}W
. This means that the schematic closure is just the topological closure with the reduced induced structure by
[
Sta22
, Lemma 056B]
. Therefore the question is purely topological, and it is straightforward using that
\mathrm{cl}^{\mathrm{sch}}_{Y}W=\mathrm{cl}_{Y}W
is the intersection of all the closed subsets
C
of
Y
that contain
W
.
∎

7.3.
Blow-ups of the moduli space of stable maps

In this section we consider a desingularization
p\colon\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
of
\mathfrak{F}
and the base change
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
. By compatibility of abelian cones with pullback,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is an open substack of
C(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}p^{*}\mathfrak{F})
. We define the main component
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
to be the closure of the smooth locus (see
Definition

7.3.2
). This definition ensures that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is open in the main component of the ambient abelian cone
C(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}p^{*}\mathfrak{F})
(
Proposition

7.2.3
), thus it is irreducible. In general,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
does not agree with the pullback of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
, which might be reducible (see
Remark

7.3.4
).
Finally, we induce a virtual fundamental class on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
.

We define

p\colon\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}\to\mathfrak{Pic}

to be any desingularization (as in
Definition

3.1.1
) of the sheaf
\mathfrak{F}
(defined in
Equation

26
). By
theorem

5.2.2
, we have a proper birational map

(37)

\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}\to\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}

where
\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Pic}
is the Rossi blow-up. We are mainly interested in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
being the Rossi or the Hu-Li blow-up (see
Section

5
).

We define

(38)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}\text{.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Note that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is proper since
p
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
are proper.
Let
p:\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
be the natural projection. Consider the Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{}\mathfrak{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{}\mathfrak{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\pi}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Pic}}

where
\mathfrak{C}
is the universal curve over
\mathfrak{Pic}
and
\mathfrak{L}
the universal line bundle. Recall that

\mathfrak{F}=R^{1}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\pi}).

Lemma 7.3.1
.

In notation as before, we have an open embedding

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,\left(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}p^{*}\mathfrak{F}\right)\cong\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,\left(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\widetilde{\pi}})\right).

Proof.

By
Proposition

7.1.2
, we have an open embedding

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\subset\mathrm{Spec}\,_{\mathfrak{Pic}}\mathrm{Sym}\,\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathfrak{F}.

Thus,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\subset\mathrm{Spec}\,_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}\mathrm{Sym}\,p^{*}\mathfrak{F}^{\oplus r+1}
and
p^{*}\mathfrak{F}\cong R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\widetilde{\pi}})
.
∎

Definition 7.3.2
.

Consider the closure in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
of the locus where the curve is smooth of genus
g
. We call this component the
main component
and we denote it by
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
.

Proposition 7.3.3
.

The following hold:

(1)

We have an open embedding

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\subset\mathrm{Spec}\,\left(\mathrm{Sym}\,\oplus_{i=0}^{r}p^{*}\mathfrak{F}\right)^{\mathrm{tf}}\simeq\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,\left(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\left(p^{*}\mathfrak{F}\right)^{\mathrm{tf}}\right).

(2)

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is proper and smooth over
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

7.3.1
we get
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\left(\mathrm{Sym}\,\oplus_{i=0}^{r}p^{*}\mathfrak{F}\right)^{\mathrm{tf}}
is an open embedding. By cohomology and base change we have
p^{*}\mathfrak{F}\simeq R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\widetilde{\pi}})
. The argument in
Proposition

7.2.3
applies to
R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\widetilde{\pi}})
and we obtain an open embedding

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,\left(\mathrm{Sym}\,\oplus_{i=0}^{r}R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\widetilde{\pi}})\right)^{\mathrm{tf}}.

By construction
(p^{*}\mathfrak{F})^{\mathrm{tf}}
is locally-free and, since the torsion-free part commutes with direct sums, the same is true for
(p^{*}\mathfrak{F}^{\oplus r+1})^{\mathrm{tf}}
. This shows that
\mathrm{Sym}\,\oplus_{i=0}^{r}(p_{k}^{*}\mathfrak{F})^{\mathrm{tf}}
is locally free therefore we have an isomorphism

\mathrm{Spec}\,\left(\mathrm{Sym}\,\oplus_{i=0}^{r}p^{*}\mathfrak{F}\right)^{\mathrm{tf}}\simeq\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,\left(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\left(p^{*}\mathfrak{F}\right)^{\mathrm{tf}}\right)

by
Lemma

6.2.1
.

The first part implies that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is smooth over
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
since it is open in a vector bundle. The properness of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
follows from the properness of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
.
∎

Remark 7.3.4
.

We consider the following Cartesian diagram:

(39)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}\text{.}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

In general,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P})
is not an isomorphism and thus the diagram below is only commutative

(40)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}\text{.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

This observation is a reflection of the fact that torsion-free part does not commute with pullback, see
Remark

6.2.2
. By pullback,
Proposition

7.2.3
induces an open embedding

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P})\subset\mathrm{Spec}\,p^{*}\left(\mathrm{Sym}\,(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathfrak{F})\right)^{\mathrm{tf}},

which in general need not factor through
\mathrm{Spec}\,\left(\mathrm{Sym}\,(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}p^{*}\mathfrak{F})\right)^{\mathrm{tf}}\not\simeq\mathrm{Spec}\,p^{*}\left(\mathrm{Sym}\,(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathfrak{F})\right)^{\mathrm{tf}}
. Meanwhile, as in the proof of
Proposition

7.2.3
, we have that

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)=\mathrm{Spec}\,\left(\mathrm{Sym}\,(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}p^{*}\mathfrak{F})\right)^{\mathrm{tf}}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d).

However,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P})
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
do give the same invariants. To see this, note that we have commuting morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{r}}

and

(41)

\overline{p}_{*}[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)]=\overline{r}_{*}[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P})]=[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)].

Let
\widehat{\pi}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
be the universal curve and let
\widehat{q}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathcal{C}
the morphism induced by
q
. The morphism
\widetilde{\mu}
has a dual perfect obstruction theory equal to

\oplus_{i=0}^{r}R^{\bullet}\widehat{\pi}_{*}\widehat{q}^{*}\mathcal{L}.

This perfect obstruction theory induces a virtual class

[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}:=\widetilde{\mu}^{!}[\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}]\in A_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)),

where
\widetilde{\mu}^{!}
is defined as in
[
Man11
]
.

Remark 7.3.5
.

While
\mathfrak{Pic}
is smooth,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
does not need to be smooth. This is not a problem, all we need for a well-defined virtual class is that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
has pure dimension. This is true, since
\mathfrak{Pic}
has pure dimension and
p
is birational.

Proposition 7.3.6
.

We have the following equality

(\overline{p})_{*}[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}=[\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}

Proof.

Note that by cohomology and base-change we have that
R^{\bullet}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\overline{p}^{*}\mathcal{L}=\overline{p}^{*}R^{\bullet}\overline{\pi}_{*}\mathcal{L}
. As
p
is birational and proper, we have
p_{*}[\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}]=[\mathfrak{Pic}]
. We now apply Costello’s Pushforward theorem (
[
HW22
]
) to
Equation

38
and we get

\overline{p}_{*}[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}=[\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}.\qed

7.4.
Definition of reduced GW invariants of hypersurfaces in all genera

In this section we define reduced Gromov–Witten invariants for hypersurfaces in projective spaces under Assumption
7.2.1
. This is less straight-forward than for projective spaces, since we have no understanding of the geometry of moduli spaces of maps to hypersurfaces.

Let
X
be a smooth hypersurface on
\mathbb{P}^{r}
defined by the vanishing of a regular section of
s
of
\mathcal{O}(k)
.
We have that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)
is cut out in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
by the vanishing of the section
\pi_{*}s
of
\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}^{\otimes k}
on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
. If
\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}^{\otimes k}
is a vector bundle, we can use this to define the virtual class of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)
by virtual pullback.
This generally fails for
g\geq 1
, so we will use the blow-ups we developed to ensure that the restriction of
\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}^{\otimes k}
to the main component
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is locally free.

Construction 7.4.1
.

Let
X\subset\mathbb{P}^{r}
be a smooth hypersurface as above.
Let
p_{k}\colon\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
be any desingularization of
\mathfrak{F}
and
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}
.

We define the main component
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)
of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)
as follows

(42)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\text{.}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

Notice that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)
may not be irreducible, but we will still refer to it as the main component. The main component
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)
does not have a perfect obstruction theory, in the following we fix this problem.

We define

(43)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
are defined in
Section

7.3
. Since they are defined over any desingularization of
\mathfrak{F}
, we can in particular replace
p:\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
by
p_{k}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
. Then
Proposition

7.3.3
implies
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)
is proper.

To sum up, we have the following diagram. Note that some of the squares are not Cartesian (See
Remark

7.3.4
)

(44)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}\text{.}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\urcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{k}}

By construction we get that
(\tilde{\pi}_{*}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes k})^{\mathrm{tf}}
is locally free on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
.

We define

(45)

[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}=i^{!}[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)].

Note that for any
1\leq j\leq n
we have morphisms

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle ev_{j}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{P}^{r}

and

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle ev_{j}}}{{\longrightarrow}}X.

By abuse of notation we denote both of these compositions by
ev_{j}
.

Remark 7.4.2
.

As in
Remark

7.3.4
we could have defined the main component of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)
to be the pull back

(46)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

As in
Remark

7.3.4
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(X)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)
may be different, but the reduced invariants with this definition would agree with the ones in
Definition

7.4.4
. The proof of this statement follows from Equation (
45
), commutativity of virtual pullbacks with proper push forwards in
[
Man11
]
and
Equation

41
.

Notice that the definition of the reduced virtual class in (
45
) does a priori depend on the choice of a desingularization of
\mathfrak{Pic}
. The following proposition shows that integration against this class does not depend on the desingularization. The proof of
Proposition

7.4.3
is delayed, and we first prove
Lemma

7.4.5
.

Proposition 7.4.3
.

Under Assumption
7.2.1
, let
p^{\prime}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
and
p^{\prime\prime}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
be birational proper maps such that

((p^{\prime})^{*}\mathfrak{F})^{\mathrm{tf}}
,
((p^{\prime\prime})^{*}\mathfrak{F})^{\mathrm{tf}}
,
((p^{\prime})^{*}(R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}))^{\mathrm{tf}}
and
((p^{\prime\prime})^{*}(R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}))^{\mathrm{tf}}
are locally free. Consider
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime\prime}
defined analogously to
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)
above. Then we have

\int_{[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime}]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\prod ev^{*}\gamma_{i}=\int_{[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime\prime}]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\prod ev^{*}\gamma_{i}

Proposition

7.4.3
permits us to define the reduced Gromov–Witten invariants as they are independent of the blowing-up of
\mathfrak{Pic}
.

Definition 7.4.4
.

For
d>2g-2
, we call
reduced
Gromov–Witten invariants of
X
, the following numbers

\int_{[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}}\prod ev^{*}\gamma_{i}.

In order to prove
Proposition

7.4.3
, we first prove the following.

Lemma 7.4.5
.

Consider a commutative diagram of Artin stacks

\textstyle{\widehat{\mathfrak{Pic}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Pic}}

and let
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
be the corresponding fiber products
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\times_{\mathfrak{Pic}}\widehat{\mathfrak{Pic}}
, respectively
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\times_{\mathfrak{Pic}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
.

(1)

We have a diagram with Cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

(2)

Suppose that
\widehat{\mathfrak{Pic}}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
are desingularizations of
\mathfrak{F}
. Let
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P},d)
be the main component of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
in the sense of
Definition

7.3.2
. Under Assumption
7.2.1
, the we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}.}

Proof.

The first statement follows from the fact that the square on the right and the big square are Cartesian. This shows that the square on the left is Cartesian.

For the second statement, we apply point 1 and we consider the following extended diagram in which all squares are Cartesian

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P}^{r})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P}^{r})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

By the definition of the main component of the moduli space of stable maps in
Definition

7.2.2
, we have solid maps in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P}^{r})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P}^{r})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d).}

The dashed arrow is the identity on maps with smooth domain. Since the map
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P}^{r})\to\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P}^{r})
is proper it maps closed substacks to closed substacks, and thus the identity map extends to a map

\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\to\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d).\qed

Proof of Proposition
7.4.3
.

Let
\widehat{\mathfrak{Pic}}
denote the closure inside the fiber product
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}^{\prime}\times_{\mathfrak{Pic}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}^{\prime\prime}
of locus of smooth curves. We then have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime\prime}}

We define
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)
by the following Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathfrak{Pic}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Pic}}

and similarly we define

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime\prime}}

Using the notation in (
43
) and
Lemma

7.4.5
, part 2, we obtain a commutative diagram

(47)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{\circ}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{p}^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{p}^{\prime}}

By
Proposition

7.3.3
we have that
\widehat{p}^{\prime}
and
\widehat{p}^{\prime\prime}
are proper. In the following we show that they are virtually birational.

Recall from
7.4.1
that we have diagrams with Cartesian squares on the left

(48)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{p}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mu}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{\prime}}

and

(49)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{p}^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mu}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{\prime\prime}}

which give

(50)

[\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{\circ}(X,d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}=(i^{\prime})^{!}[\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)]\quad\text{and}\quad[\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{\circ}(X,d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}=(i^{\prime\prime})^{!}[\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)].

Since
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{\circ}(X,d)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\prime}
are irreducible and have an isomorphic open subset, we have that
r^{\prime}
and
r^{\prime\prime}
are birational. By
Proposition

7.3.3
they are also proper and thus we have

(51)

\displaystyle r^{\prime}_{*}[\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)]=[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\prime}]\quad\text{ and}\quad r^{\prime\prime}_{*}[\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)]=[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\prime\prime}].

Using (
50
), (
51
) and commutativity of pullbacks with push-forwards in
eq.

48
and
eq.

49
, we get

(52)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{p}^{\prime}_{*}[\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{\circ}(X,d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}&=[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime}]^{\mathrm{vir}}\quad\text{\ and}\\
\widehat{p}^{\prime\prime}_{*}[\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}^{\circ}(X,d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}&=[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)^{\prime\prime}]^{\mathrm{vir}}.\end{split}

Intersecting both equations above with
\prod ev^{*}\gamma_{i}
we get the conclusion.
∎

In the following we discuss several ways of blowing up
\mathfrak{Pic}
to desingularize
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}
.

Lemma 7.4.6
.

Given
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{G}
sheaves on an integral scheme
X
, with
\mathcal{G}
torsion free and
f:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
a morphism, we have that
f
factors through

\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{tf}}\to\mathcal{G}.

Proof.

Since
X
is integral and
\mathcal{G}
is torsion free, we have that the composition
{\rm Tor}(\mathcal{F})\to\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
is zero. The claim now follows from the universal property of quotients.
∎

Proposition 7.4.7
.

In notation of
Section

7.3
,

we have the following:

(1)

An isomorphism

\psi:(p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})^{\mathrm{tf}}\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}.

(2)

\mathrm{Bl}_{R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}}\mathfrak{Pic}
is a desingularization of
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}
, for any integer
k>0
.

Proof.

1. As in
Remark

7.1.4
, let
A
a section of
\mathfrak{C}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
such that
R^{1}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}(A))=0
. By abuse of notation we denote by
A
the pull back of
A
to
\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}
. We have exact sequences on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
which fit into a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}(A))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}(A)|_{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(A))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(A)|_{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}

where the vertical arrows are obtained by cohomology and base change and the solid arrows are isomorphisms.

By
Lemma

7.4.6
, we have a morphism

\psi:(p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})^{\mathrm{tf}}\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}

which sits in a commutative diagram

(53)

\textstyle{p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})^{\mathrm{tf}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}(A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(A)).}

Since the Image of the map
(p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})^{\mathrm{tf}}\to p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}(A))
is equal to the kernel of
p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}(A))\to p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}(A)|_{A})
, and the Image of
R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(A)
, is equal to the kernel of
R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(A))\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(A)|_{A})
, the isomorphisms in the diagram show that

(54)

{\rm Im}\left((p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})^{\mathrm{tf}}\to p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}(A))\right)\simeq{\rm Im}\left(R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}(A))\right).

Since
p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}\to p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}(A))
and
p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}
are generically injective we have that
(p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})^{\mathrm{tf}}\to p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}(A))
and
(p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})^{\mathrm{tf}}\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}
are injective. This together with
54
shows that
\psi
is an isomorphism.

2. Without loss of generality we assume that
k=1
. Let
p:\mathrm{Bl}_{R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}}\mathfrak{Pic}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
denote the projection. By cohomology and base change we have
p^{*}(R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})\simeq R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}
. With this, we have that
(R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}})^{\mathrm{tf}}
is locally free.

Since

\left((R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}})^{\mathrm{tf}}\right)^{\vee}\simeq(R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}})^{\vee}

and
(R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}})^{\mathrm{tf}}
is locally free, we get that
(R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}})^{\vee}
is locally free.

By
Proposition

7.1.3
we have that
R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}\simeq(R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}})^{\vee}
. This together with the above shows that
R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}
is locally free. The claim now follows from the first part of the proposition.
∎

Proposition 7.4.8
.

In notation

of
Section

7.3
, we have that
\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}
is a desingularization of
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}
and
R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}
.

Proof.

We first prove that
\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}
is a desingularization of
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}
for
k=1
. By cohomology and base change we have
p^{*}R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\simeq R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}/\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
. By
Proposition

7.1.3
we have
(R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}/\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}})^{\vee}\simeq R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}
. By
Proposition

7.4.7
we have
R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}\simeq(p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})^{\mathrm{tf}}
. With these we get

\left(\left(p^{*}R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\right)^{\mathrm{tf}}\right)^{\vee}\simeq\left(p^{*}R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\right)^{\vee}\simeq(p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})^{\mathrm{tf}}.

This shows that
\left(p^{*}R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\right)^{\mathrm{tf}}
is locally free implies
(p^{*}R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L})^{\mathrm{tf}}
is locally free. Notice that in general the dual does not commute with pullback, so the above argument was needed.

In the following we show that
\mathrm{Bl}_{R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}}\mathfrak{Pic}
is a desingularization of
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}
, for any
k>0
.

Locally we choose
B
a section such that
\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}\simeq\mathfrak{L}(B)
. Taking
A
such that
R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)=0
, we have a diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cdot A}
\textstyle{R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cdot B}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A+B).}

Let
U
be the subset of
\mathfrak{Pic}
, where
R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}=0
. Since we work under the assumption
7.2.1
,
U
is a non-empty open subset. Then on
U
we have the following exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\to R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}\to

\displaystyle R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(B)\to R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(B)|_{B}\to 0

(55)

\displaystyle 0\to R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)\to

\displaystyle R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A+B)\to R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A+B)|_{B}\to 0.

By possibly shrinking
U
, the section
A
may be chosen to avoid
B
. With this, we have that multiplication with
A
induces an isomorphism

R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(B)|_{B}\simeq R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A+B)|_{B}.

By possibly shrinking
U
, we may assume that
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A)
and
R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}(A+B)
are trivial, and that the sequences in
7.4
are split. The claim now follows from
Corollary

3.5.5
.

By the proof above, we have that
R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\otimes k}
is locally free. This shows that
R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\otimes k}
is locally free. By cohomology and base change, we have that
(R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\otimes k})^{\mathrm{tf}}\simeq(p^{*}R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k})^{\mathrm{tf}}
and thus
\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}
is a desingularization of
R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}
.
∎

In genus one, following
[
VZ08
,
HL10
]
, one can define reduced Gromov-Witten invariants of degree-
k
hypersurfaces on
\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}
. Below we give a direct proof of this fact. The proof below does not generalise to higher genus.

Proposition 7.4.9
.

Let
g=1
. Then for every
k\geq 1
, the sheaf
\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\mathrm{ev}^{*}\mathcal{O}(k)
is locally free on the main component
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,n}^{\circ}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
.

Proof.

Fix
k\geq 1
. By
Equation

66
, we need to show that
R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k})
is locally free over the image
Z^{\circ}
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
via the forgetful morphism.

In a neighbourhood of
(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L})\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{1}
we can choose a section
A
of
\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k-1}
. This gives an exact sequence

(56)

0\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot A}}{{\longrightarrow}}R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\otimes k})\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\otimes k}|_{A})\to R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot A}}{{\rightarrow}}R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\otimes k})\to 0.

If
\tilde{L}
is non-negative on each component of
\tilde{C}
, as is the case in
Z^{\circ}
, we have the equality

h^{1}(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L})=h^{1}(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L}^{\otimes k})

by a Riemann-Roch computation. Since cohomology in degree 1 commutes with base change and the map
H^{1}(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L})\to H^{1}(\widetilde{C},\widetilde{L}^{\otimes k})
is an isomorphism, we have that the last arrow in sequence (
56
) is an isomorphism. This shows that we have a short exact sequence

0\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\otimes k})\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\otimes k}|_{A})\to 0.

Since
R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}|_{A})
is locally free and by
Proposition

7.4.7
the sheaf
R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}\simeq(R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}})^{\vee}
is also locally free on
Z^{\circ}
, we get that
R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{L}}^{\otimes k})
is locally free.
∎

Remark 7.4.10
.

Above we denoted by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
any desingularization of
\mathfrak{F}
. We collect here various blow-ups of interest.

(1)

\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}
in the sense of Rossi (see
Section

5.1
)

(2)

\mathrm{Bl}^{HL}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}
in the sense of Hu–Li.

(3)

\mathrm{Bl}_{R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}}\mathfrak{Pic}

(4)

\mathrm{Bl}_{R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}}\mathfrak{Pic}

(5)

\mathrm{Bl}^{HL}_{R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}}\mathrm{Bl}^{HL}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}

We have

\mathrm{Bl}^{HL}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}\to\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{Bl}^{HL}_{R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}}\mathrm{Bl}^{HL}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}\to\mathrm{Bl}_{R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}}\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}.

By
Proposition

7.4.8
and by
proposition

7.4.7
we have

\mathrm{Bl}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}\to\mathrm{Bl}_{R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}}\mathfrak{Pic}\to Bl_{R^{0}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}}\mathfrak{Pic}.

7.5.
Reduced invariants from stable maps with fields

Reduced invariants are conjecturally related to Gromov–Witten invariants
[
Zin09a
]
,
[
HL11
, Conjecture 1.1]
. One of the main difficulties in proving such conjectures is that one needs to understand how to split the virtual class of a moduli space of stable maps among its irreducible components. What makes this task particularly difficult is that almost nothing is known about the geometry of this moduli space of stable maps.

In genus one and two the existing algebraic proofs
[
CL15
,
LLO22
,
LO21
,
LO22
]
use an additional well-behaved moduli space of maps with fields
[
CL12
]
. In view of these conjectures, we discuss blow-ups of maps with fields and we show that the ingredients for the low genus proof are also available in higher genus.

Given
X
a hypersurface (or more generally a complete intersection) in
\mathbb{P}^{r}
, the associated moduli space of maps with fields has the following features:

(1)

in genus one and two it has well-understood geometry, such as local equations and irreducible components (see
[
HL10
,
HLN12
]
);

(2)

it has a virtual class and a localised virtual class (see
[
KL13
]
, Definition 3.3); the latter is needed because the space of maps with fields is
not compact
;

(3)

the localised virtual class is supported on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)
and it coincides up to a sign to the virtual class of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)
(see
[
KL13
]
, Theorem 1.1).

These properties allow us to work with the well-understood moduli space of stable maps with fields instead of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)
, whose geometry is unavailable.

In the following we blow up the moduli space of maps with fields, to define reduced invariants in this context. The main theorem of this section,
Theorem

7.5.3
, shows that the blown-up moduli space of maps with fields also has property (1) listed above. In future work we will also investigate the (intrinsic) normal cone of the space of maps with fields.

7.5.1.
Review of maps with fields

We recall the construction and properties of the moduli space of maps with
p
-fields. This space was introduced in
[
CL12
]
to study higher genus Gromov-Witten invariants of the quintic threefold.

In the following we fix
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
k>1
, and we consider the sheaf

\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}^{\oplus r+1}\oplus(\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes-k}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}))

on
\mathfrak{Pic}
and its corresponding abelian cone

S\left(\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}^{\oplus r+1}\oplus(\mathfrak{L}^{{}^{\otimes-k}}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}})\right)=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,R^{1}\pi_{*}\left(\left(\mathfrak{L}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}}\right)^{\oplus r+1}\oplus\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu^{p}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{Pic}.

Recall that, in our notation, we have already imposed on
\mathfrak{Pic}=\mathfrak{Pic}^{\rm st}_{g,n,d}
the stability condition in
Equation

23
. Then, the
moduli space of maps with
p
-fields
is defined in
[
CL12
, Section 3.1]
as an open in
the abelian cone

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}=S\left(\pi_{*}(\mathfrak{L}^{\oplus r+1}\oplus(\mathfrak{L}^{{}^{\otimes-k}}\otimes\omega_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{Pic}})\right)

Therefore, an element of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
over
(C,L,\underline{s})\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is given by a choice of a section
p\in H^{0}(C,L^{\otimes-k}\otimes\omega_{C})
.

Consider the Cartesian diagram

(57)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\text{.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\pi}^{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\pi}}

The complex

(58)

\mathbb{E}_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}/\mathfrak{Pic}}:=R^{\bullet}\overline{\pi}^{p}_{*}(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathcal{L}\oplus\mathcal{L}^{\otimes-k}\otimes\omega_{\overline{\pi}^{p}})

is a dual obstruction theory for the morphism
\mu^{p}
.
The stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
is not proper, but the perfect obstruction theory admits a cosection
\sigma
, that is, a morphism

\sigma\colon h^{1}(\mathbb{E}^{\vee})\to\mathcal{O}_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}}.

Note that since
\mathfrak{Pic}
s smooth we have that the absolute obstruction of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
is isomorphic to
h^{1}(\mathbb{E}^{\vee})
and the cosection lifts (see
[
CL12
, Proposition 3.5]
) . This data gives a cosection localised virtual class

[\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\sigma}
.

For
X
a smooth subvariety cut out by any regular section of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{r}}(k)
,
[
CL20
, Theorem 1.1]
states that

(59)

[\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\sigma}=(-1)^{(r+1)d+1-g}[\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in A_{d^{\mathrm{vir}}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d))}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d))

The particular case where
r=4
and
k=5
(therefore
X
is a quintic threefold) was the motivation for introducing
p
-fields in the first place. In
[
CL12
, Theorem 1.1]
, the authors proved
Equation

59
at the level of invariants before it was upgraded to classes in
[
CL20
]
.

7.5.2.
Blow-ups of maps with fields

In this section we discuss a non-minimal blow-up which has good properties.

In notation as before we consider

(60)

\mathfrak{E}_{k}:=R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}.

We define

(61)

\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}:=\mathrm{Bl}^{HL}_{\mathfrak{E}_{k}}\mathrm{Bl}^{HL}_{\mathfrak{F}}\mathfrak{Pic}.

Similarly to
Equation

38
, we define
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
as the following Cartesian diagram:

(62)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}\text{.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}^{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{p}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{k}}

Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
is an open substack of
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,p_{k}^{*}(\mathfrak{E}_{k}\oplus\mathfrak{F}^{\oplus r+1})
, we have a perfect obstruction theory for
\widetilde{\mu}^{p}
exactly analogous to that in (
58
).

Indeed, let
\widehat{\pi}:\widetilde{C}^{p}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
be the base-change of the universal curve of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
, and
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}
be pullback of the line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathcal{C}^{p}
. The perfect obstruction theory on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
is given by

\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}/\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}}=\overline{p}_{k}^{*}\mathbb{E}_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}/\mathfrak{Pic}}=R^{\bullet}\widehat{\pi}_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\oplus r+1}\oplus\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes-k}\otimes\omega_{\widehat{\pi}})

The cosection
\sigma
induces

\widetilde{\sigma}=\overline{p}_{k}^{*}\sigma:h^{1}(\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}/\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}})\to\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}}.

One then obtains a localized virtual class as in
[
CL12
]
, with the additional feature that our
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
may be singular. The argument there can be adapted to our case with minimal changes. We sketch the argument below, while pointing out the differences.

We do not have an absolute obstruction theory, but only one relative to
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
, so we do not have a cosection of the absolute obstruction theory. The (absolute) cosection is only needed to prove that the intrinsic normal cone is contained in the kernel of the cosection. Instead of lifting the cosection as in
[
CL12
]
, we work relatively to
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}
and we show that normal cone of
\widetilde{\mu}^{p}
is contained in the kernel cone
E(\widetilde{\sigma})
.

For
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
, the fact that the normal cone of
\mu^{p}
is contained in
E(\sigma)
is guaranteed by
[
CL12
, Proposition 3.5]
. Using the diagram in
Equation

62
and
[
Man11
, Remark 3.5]
we have that

\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mu}^{p}}\hookrightarrow\overline{p}_{k}^{*}\mathfrak{C}_{\mu^{p}}.

As in
[
KL13
, Corollary 4.5]
, it is enough to show that
\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mu}^{p}}\hookrightarrow\overline{p}_{k}^{*}E(\sigma)
on the subset where
\widetilde{\sigma}
is surjective. With this, we have
E(\widetilde{\sigma})=\overline{p}_{k}^{*}E(\sigma)
. This shows that

\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mu}^{p}}\hookrightarrow E(\widetilde{\sigma}),

as required. The rest of the construction follows as in the classical case. Intersecting the normal cone of
\widetilde{\mu}^{p}
with the zero section of
E(\widetilde{\sigma})
, we obtain localised virtual class
[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]_{\widetilde{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{vir}}
supported on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)\times_{\mathfrak{Pic}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}
. See
[
CL12
, Section 3]
for details.

Since
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
by zero section, we have
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
. Under the assumption in
7.2.1
,
we have that
R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}^{\otimes k}=0
. We define the main component of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
to be
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
.
Under assumption
7.2.1
, the fibers of the cone

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)\times_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)

are trivial and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is indeed a component of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
; in fact, it agrees with the main component of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
as an abelian cone. Even though geometrically the main components of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
agree, the second one carries a non-trivial virtual structure in the form of an obstruction sheaf which is the restriction of
R^{1}\widehat{\pi}_{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes-k}\otimes\omega_{\widehat{\pi}}
as well as a cosection.

Theorem 7.5.3
.

Denote by
(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p,\lambda})_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
the irreducible components of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
and
(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\theta})_{\theta\in\Theta}
the irreducible components of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
.

Let

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}^{p,\lambda}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\to\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p,\lambda}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}^{\theta}:\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\to\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\theta}

the pull-backs of the universal curve on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
.
The following statements hold.

(1)

The morphism
\bar{p}_{k}
of (
62
) is proper.

(2)

The irreducible components
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p,\lambda}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\theta}
are smooth over their image in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}
. In particular,
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
is smooth over
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}
.

(3)

The sheaf
\widehat{\pi}^{p,\lambda}_{*}ev^{*}\mathcal{O}(k)
is a locally free sheaf on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p,\lambda}
, the sheaf
\widetilde{\pi}^{\theta}_{*}ev^{*}\mathcal{O}(k)
is a locally free sheaf on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\theta}
. In particular,
\widetilde{\pi}^{\circ}_{*}ev^{*}\mathcal{O}(k)
is a locally free sheaf on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
.

Proof.

1. We have that
\overline{p}_{k}
is proper, as
p_{k}
is proper.

2. Consider the following diagram

(63)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,p_{k}^{*}(\mathfrak{F}^{\oplus r+1}\oplus\mathfrak{E}_{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,\mathfrak{F}^{\oplus r+1}\oplus\mathfrak{E}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}\text{.}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{k}}

We have that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
is an open substack of
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,p_{k}^{*}(\mathfrak{E}_{k}\oplus\mathfrak{F}^{\oplus r+1})
and by
Theorem

6.3.1
we have that the irreducible components of the stacks

\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,\ \mathfrak{F}^{\oplus r+1}\oplus\mathfrak{E}_{k}\text{\ \ \ and \ \ \ }\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,\mathfrak{F}^{\oplus r+1}

are smooth over their image in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}
.

This shows that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p,\lambda}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\theta}
are smooth over their image in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}
. In particular

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)=\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathrm{Sym}\,(p_{k}^{*}\mathfrak{F}^{\oplus r+1})^{\mathrm{tf}}\mbox{ is smooth over }\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}.

3. Let
\mu^{\lambda}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p,\lambda}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
be the restriction of
\mu^{p}
. Let
Z^{\lambda}
be the image of
\mu^{\lambda}
. Let
\pi^{\lambda}:\mathfrak{C}^{\lambda}\to Z^{\lambda}
be the restriction of
\pi
. Let
\widetilde{Z}^{\lambda}
be the fiber product

(64)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Z}^{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ Z^{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Pic}\text{.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{k}^{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{k}}

Let
\widetilde{\pi}^{\lambda}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}^{\lambda}\to Z^{\lambda}
be the restriction of

\widetilde{\pi}
and let
q_{k}^{\lambda}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}^{\lambda}\to\mathfrak{C}^{\lambda}
be the restriction of
q_{k}
. By commutativity of proper push-forwards with base-change we have that

(p_{k}^{\lambda})^{*}R^{\bullet}\pi^{\lambda}_{*}\mathfrak{L}\simeq R^{\bullet}\widetilde{\pi}^{\lambda}_{*}(q_{k}^{\lambda})^{*}\mathfrak{L}

Again, cohomology and base-change in the Cartesian diagram

(65)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ \widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}^{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Z}^{\lambda}\text{.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\pi}^{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\pi}^{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{\lambda}}

gives

(66)

R^{\bullet}\widehat{\pi}^{\lambda}_{*}ev^{*}\mathcal{O}(k)=(\mu^{\lambda})^{*}R^{\bullet}\widetilde{\pi}^{\lambda}_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}.

We have a short exact sequence

0\to R^{0}\widetilde{\pi}^{\lambda}_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{k}\to E^{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\rightarrow}}E^{1}\to R^{1}\widetilde{\pi}^{\lambda}_{*}\mathfrak{L}^{\otimes k}\to 0.

By construction we have that
\phi
is locally diagonal. By
Equation

66
we have that

R^{\bullet}\widehat{\pi}^{\lambda}_{*}ev^{*}\mathcal{O}(k)\simeq[(\mu^{\lambda})^{*}E^{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mu^{\lambda})^{*}\phi}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\mu^{\lambda})^{*}E^{1}].

Since
(\mu^{\lambda})^{*}\phi
is locally diagonal,
Proposition

4.1.3
implies that
R^{0}\widehat{\pi}^{\lambda}_{*}ev^{*}\mathcal{O}(k)
is locally free.

A similar argument shows that
\widetilde{\pi}^{\theta}_{*}ev^{*}\mathcal{O}(k)
is a locally free sheaf on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{\theta}
and in particular
\widetilde{\pi}^{\circ}_{*}ev^{*}\mathcal{O}(k)
is a locally free sheaf on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
.
∎

Proposition 7.5.4
.

The localised invariants do not depend on the blow-up of
\mathfrak{Pic}
, more precisely,

(\overline{p}_{k})_{*}[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{vir}}=[\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{vir}}.

Proof.

The arguments used in
[
CL12
]
and its various generalizations to establish
Equation

59
for the cosection localized virtual class on the right hand-side can be adapted in a straight-forward manner to establish an analogous result for the class on the left-hand side. That is,

(67)

[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]_{\widetilde{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{vir}}=(-1)^{(r+1)d+1-g}[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}.

The only issue with adapting the classical proof comes from the fact that the cosection
\widetilde{\sigma}
does not lift to the absolute obstruction theory. The discussion a the beginning of
Section

7.5.2
shows how to get around this requirement, since in our set-up all the stacks, obstruction theories and cosections are induced by base-changing the usual ones over the morphism
p_{k}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}\to\mathfrak{Pic}
.
Let

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)=\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)\times_{\mathfrak{Pic}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}

with
pr_{1}:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)
. Now
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)
can be given a perfect obstruction theory by pulling back that of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)
, and similarly to
Proposition

7.3.6
we have

(68)

(pr_{1})_{*}[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}=[\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(X,d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}.

Then the result follows from
Equation

68
,
Equation

59
and
Equation

67
.
∎

7.5.5.
Reduced invariants from maps with fields

Let
\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}/\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}}
denote the relative intrinsic normal cone of the morphism
\widetilde{\mu}^{p}:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}

[
BF96
, Section 7]
. We have
\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}/\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}=\cup_{i}\mathfrak{C}_{i}
, where
\mathfrak{C}_{i}
are its (finitely many) irreducible components. By the discussion above, the main component of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
, which is smooth over
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}
. So there is only one component of the intrinsic normal cone which is supported over an open of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
, which we denote by
\mathfrak{C}_{0}
.
The cosection-localized virtual class of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
is defined as

[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]_{\widetilde{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{vir}}=0_{\widetilde{\sigma}}^{!}[\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}/\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}_{k}}]

where
0_{\widetilde{\sigma}}^{!}
denotes the cosection-localized virtual Gysin pullback.
Then,

[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]_{\widetilde{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{vir}}=\sum_{i}[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]_{i}^{\mathrm{vir}}

for
[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]_{i}^{\mathrm{vir}}
the class corresponding to the component
\mathfrak{C}_{i}
. In particular,
[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]_{0}^{\mathrm{vir}}
is supported over
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)
.

Proposition 7.5.6
.

Denote by
[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]_{0}^{\mathrm{vir}}
the cosection-localized virtual fundamental class corresponding to the cone
\mathfrak{C}_{0}
.
We have

[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)]^{\mathrm{vir}}=(-1)^{kd-g+1}[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}]_{0}^{\mathrm{vir}}\in A_{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(X,d)).

Proof.

This follows the lines of proof of Corollary 4.4 in
[
CL15
]
. Let

\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\bullet}:=R^{\bullet}\widehat{\pi}^{p}_{*}(\oplus_{i=0}^{r}\mathcal{L}),\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbb{E}_{2}^{\bullet}:=R^{\bullet}\widehat{\pi}^{p}_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\otimes-k}\otimes\omega_{\widehat{\pi}^{p}})

and let
\mathcal{E}_{i}=h^{1}/h^{0}(\mathbb{E}_{i}^{\bullet})
and
\mathcal{E}=h^{1}/h^{0}(\mathbb{E}^{\bullet})
. We have that
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is a vector bundle stack on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
and
\mathcal{E}\simeq\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2}
. Let
\mathcal{U}
be the open subset of the main component of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}
, with consists of maps with fields with irreducible source. On
\mathcal{U}
we have
R^{1}\widehat{\pi}_{*}f^{*}\mathcal{O}(k)=0
, and thus
\mathcal{U}
is also an open subset of
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\circ}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)
. Using that
\mathcal{U}
is smooth and unobstructed, we see that
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{U}/\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}
is isomorphic to the vector bundle stack
\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{\mathcal{U}}
. Since the embedding
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{U}/\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}\hookrightarrow h^{1}/h^{0}(\mathcal{E}|_{\mathcal{U}})
is

(\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus 0)|_{\mathcal{U}}\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2})|_{\mathcal{U}}

and
\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}(\mathbb{P}^{r},d)^{p}/\widetilde{\mathfrak{Pic}}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}
is a closed embedding, we get that
\mathfrak{C}_{0}\simeq\mathcal{E}_{1}
. By the definition of the localised cosection virtual class, we get
